16:I[44692,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DailyView"]
17:T461,Hyper-Connections maintain n parallel residual streams. A sublayer reads them through a pre-map, applies attention or an FFN, writes the result back through a post-map, and transports the existing residual state through a learned matrix H_res. Standard HC leaves H_res unconstrained, so its singular values can cause exploding or vanishing transport. mHC projects H_res onto the Birkhoff polytope of doubly stochastic matrices, usually with Sinkhorn-Knopp iterations. This caps the largest gain at one, but does not lower-bound the smallest gain. The paper decomposes stream states into a mean component and inter-stream difference components, showing that doubly stochastic transport preserves the mean while shrinking differences. Identity HC avoids this shrinkage but removes cross-stream mixing. oHC instead constrains H_res to SO(n), the group of rotations. This keeps all singular values equal to one, so the transport is norm-preserving in every direction while still mixing streams. For four streams, the paper gives an exact SO(4) parameterization using a pair of unit quaternions, avoiding iterative projection.18:T4ab,The method is called Declarative Attention (DA). The model is prompted to emit parseable control tags inside its chain-of-thought, declaring the attention scope it needs at each phase of generation. An inference-side state machine reads these tags online and constructs the attention mask dynamically. DA defines three modes. <global> allows access to the full context and is used when the model needs to survey or search broadly. <focus> commits the model to a specific region of the context, restricting attention to that region. <local> limits attention to recent generated tokens, avoiding long-context reads altogether. This is the key departure from prior sparse-attention methods: the selection signal comes from the model’s own generated reasoning trace, not from hidden-state proxy scorers, recency heuristics, historical attention statistics, or an auxiliary scan over the cache. The paper also includes a practical implementation in vLLM using block-aligned, in-place KV-cache masking compatible with FlashAttention. Another notable point is that the protocol works zero-shot on off-the-shelf models, without task-specific fine-tuning or hand-designed partitions for each benchmark.19:T4cc,The evaluation covers 15 long-context tasks, with main results reported on Gemma-4-31B and Qwen-3.6-27B. The central finding is a favorable cost-accuracy trade-off. DA reduces average decoding attention cost by 52.0% on Gemma-4-31B with only a 1.27 percentage-point accuracy drop, and by 31.1% on Qwen-3.6-27B with a 2.75-point drop. The paper also shows positive scaling behavior: as model size increases from 4B to 31B, the accuracy gap between DA and full attention narrows, suggesting that self-declared attention depends on model capability. Absolute savings grow with context length, reaching up to 21 million attended tokens saved per response. The ablations are important. Relative to a maskless ablation, the DA-induced dynamic mask cuts attended tokens by as much as 71.1%, indicating that the savings are driven by the masking mechanism itself rather than by prompt formatting alone. On the systems side, a roofline-style analysis estimates decode wall-clock cost reductions to 0.71× of vanilla for Gemma-4-31B and 0.77× for Qwen-3.6-27B on a well-optimized serving stack. The paper is careful not to equate token savings with end-to-end speedup directly; it grounds the estimate in memory-bandwidth considerations.1a:T491,Begin with the introduction, especially the discussion of why long-context decoding is bandwidth-bound due to repeated KV-cache reads. Then read the DA protocol section closely to understand the semantics of <global>, <focus>, and <local>, and how the inference-side state machine turns generated tags into masks. In the experiments, start with the main 15-task results to get the overall cost-accuracy picture, then read the scaling section because it reveals whether the protocol improves with model capability. After that, focus on the ablations, especially the maskless comparison and any analyses of mode transitions, since those are the best tests of whether the gains come from dynamic masking rather than just extra CoT structure. Finally, read the vLLM implementation and roofline analysis to judge practical viability. Three questions are worth tracking while reading: how well declared focus regions align with actual useful context; whether the protocol can induce premature narrowing of attention; and how much additional gain might be unlocked by explicit post-training for DA. A careful pass through the core sections should take around 50 to 70 minutes.1b:T686,The method reformulates ordinal decoding as a linear assignment problem. A single prefill pass over the user context and candidate items produces hidden representations for all items. A lightweight self-attention scoring head then maps those hidden states to an N×K item-position score matrix M, where Mij measures the affinity of item i for rank position j. Instead of generating ordinals one token at a time, the model solves for the maximum-weight bipartite matching between items and positions using the Hungarian algorithm or LAPJV, which directly yields a valid permutation.

This design makes several deliberate choices. It reads scores from prefill hidden states rather than output logits, based on the claim that the comparative information needed for ranking is already encoded by the backbone’s self-attention during prefill. It delegates permutation validity to the solver rather than asking the model to learn it, so duplicate or missing ordinals cannot occur. And it keeps the additional head lightweight, so the method removes sequential decode cost without introducing another heavy model.

The paper also compares multiple scoring-head variants and analyzes the role of self-attention in the readout stage. That is an important detail. If a simple independent head were enough, the ranking signal would be mostly item-wise. If a self-attention head helps substantially, then extracting the permutation still requires modeling competition among candidates even after prefill. The paper’s framing suggests the latter, which is why the contribution is more than just parallel classification: it is a structured decoding formulation tied to assignment.1c:T5a9,The headline result is roughly a 64× speedup over an autoregressive LLM on both proprietary data and Amazon Beauty, while maintaining similar ranking quality. One latency breakdown is especially informative: with an explicit reasoning trace, the teacher takes about 1807 ms per request, of which only about 28 ms is prefill. That means the overwhelming majority of time is spent in sequential decoding. Without reasoning, the teacher emits about 39 tokens; with reasoning, about 657 tokens. This makes clear how badly decode cost can scale when a generative ranker produces intermediate text in addition to the final ordering.

The most useful experimental comparisons are: teacher AR decoding versus hLLM on the quality-latency trade-off, ablations over different scoring heads, and analyses isolating the contributions of the backbone and the self-attention layers. The central empirical question is whether a single set of prefill hidden states is sufficient to recover the teacher’s ranking. The paper’s answer is largely yes, though the fidelity depends on the readout head and training setup.

One caveat is that the reported speedup applies mainly to the reranking decode stage, not necessarily to the full retrieval-plus-ranking system. Also, the assignment solver is polynomial-time, but for typical slate sizes it is far cheaper than hundreds of autoregressive decode steps. That distinction matters when interpreting the 64× figure.1d:T427,For a skim, start with the introduction and problem formulation. The two numbers to remember are that prefill is cheap and decode is expensive, and that the output is a permutation rather than free-form text. Then go straight to the score-matrix construction and assignment decoding in the method section; that is the conceptual core. In the experiments, prioritize the latency breakdown, the quality comparison against the AR teacher, and the ablations over scoring heads. The main thread can be read in about 20–30 minutes.

If the goal is to judge whether the idea generalizes beyond reranking, focus on how the paper argues that prefill hidden states already contain the ranking information. That claim is more important than the exact ranking metric. Also pay attention to whether the self-attention head is truly necessary. If it clearly outperforms simpler heads, that suggests structured output readout still needs explicit modeling of candidate interactions. That is the part most relevant to broader work on non-AR decoding and constrained generation.1e:T563,XMerge has two components. The first is cross-axis selection. Each block is scored along two axes: relative magnitude, which measures the size of the residual displacement, and Block-Influence, which measures directional change between input and output hidden states. The two scores are standardized across eligible layers and combined with a maximum. A block is considered quiet only when neither axis is large. This max rule is intentional: an average could hide a large angular change with a small magnitude score, or the reverse. The second component is reconstructive layer merging. Instead of dropping a layer, analytically folding weights, or adding a learned replacement module, XMerge distills the original adjacent two-block mapping into one existing standard Transformer block. For calibration inputs, it caches the pair input h_l and the original pair output h_{l+2}, then optimizes all parameters of the surviving block so that the single block maps h_l directly to h_{l+2}. After k merges, the result is an ordinary L-k-layer Transformer with no extra inference-time parameters, no task labels, and no end-to-end fine-tuning. The closest contrast is with methods such as ReplaceMe, LLM-Streamline, and analytic merge/fold approaches; XMerge is distinctive because it reconstructs with a full existing nonlinear standard block while preserving the serving interface.1f:T6a2,The method has three main ingredients. First, the authors approximate the Hessian with the empirical Fisher, using the standard assumption that a converged pretrained model is near a local optimum. They reshape the full gradient vector into a matrix G and show that the Fisher admits an exact decomposition as a sum of Kronecker products. After expressing the relevant expectation in terms of E[G⊗G], they take its SVD and recover a representation of the form J(w)=Σ_i σ_i U_i⊗V_i. The approximation comes from truncating this sum to the top-r components.

Second, they avoid explicitly constructing the enormous n^2 by m^2 matrix. Instead, they use an implicitly restarted Arnoldi solver that only needs matrix-vector products. The key identity is (G⊗G)vec(V)=vec(GVG^T), which turns the expensive operation into smaller dense multiplications on reshaped gradients. This is what makes the method scalable.

Third, they do not rely on the low-rank Kronecker term alone. They compute the Fisher diagonal exactly and combine it with the truncated decomposition. This hybrid design is sensible: the low-rank term captures correlations, while the exact diagonal preserves per-parameter scale. In practice, that should be more stable than either a pure diagonal approximation or a pure low-rank one.

Compared with K-FAC-style approximations, block-diagonal Hessians, or layerwise curvature proxies used in quantization, the distinguishing feature is the explicit attempt to preserve cross-layer interactions. The paper is not mainly about within-layer second-order structure. It is about whether off-diagonal coupling is strong enough to predict super-additive damage under joint perturbations.20:T868,The experiments span four LLM families from 350M to 7B parameters. Even without the full tables in the provided text, the main empirical findings are clear.

First, the paper reports direct evidence of substantial non-diagonal Fisher/Hessian structure in large language models. This matters because it validates, at realistic scale, the claim that diagonal approximations miss important information. Across multiple model families, value projection layers emerge as the most sensitive components and also show the strongest cross-layer correlations. Other module types behave less uniformly and appear more architecture-dependent.

Second, the proposed curvature scores correlate strongly with compression damage. The authors evaluate quantization and sparsification and show that layerwise Fisher/Hessian values track performance degradation well, better than cruder independent-layer approximations. In other words, layers with larger curvature are more likely to cause larger perplexity or task-performance drops when perturbed.

Third, they run inter-layer corruption experiments rather than only single-layer ablations. When two strongly coupled layers are compressed or corrupted together, the resulting degradation exceeds the sum of their individual effects. The off-diagonal curvature terms predict this excess damage. This is one of the most important results in the paper, because it shows that cross-layer structure is not just mathematically elegant; it explains real failure modes.

Fourth, they study post-corruption recovery. After damaging selected layers, they fine-tune different candidate layers and find that updating the layers most strongly coupled to the corrupted ones yields the best recovery. That is a practical result: the best repair target is not always the damaged layer itself, but sometimes the layer most tightly coupled to it.

The numbers worth remembering are the scale range, 350M to 7B, and the reduction from quadratic to linear memory complexity. The evaluation is also broader than a single compression setting: quantization, sparsification, joint corruption, and post-corruption fine-tuning are all included.21:T566,This is a skim paper, not one that requires line-by-line derivation. Focus on three parts.

First, read the method section for the main chain of ideas: why the Fisher can be written as a sum of Kronecker products, what exactly is approximated by truncation, and how Arnoldi avoids explicit matrix construction. If those three points are clear, the scalability story is clear.

Second, inspect the figures that visualize non-diagonal structure and layer coupling. Those are the core evidence that the method goes beyond diagonal or block-diagonal approximations. Pay particular attention to whether the value projection finding is stable across model families.

Third, read the joint corruption and post-corruption fine-tuning sections carefully. Those experiments are where cross-layer terms prove their practical value. A simple correlation with single-layer quantization damage would not be enough, because many weaker proxies could also achieve that.

A reasonable reading budget is 25–35 minutes. If time is tight, read the introduction, the method overview, the main experimental figures, and the complexity discussion. Two follow-up questions are worth keeping in mind: how reliable the empirical Fisher is as a Hessian proxy for pretrained LMs, and whether this low-rank Kronecker structure can be turned into a compression algorithm rather than remaining an analysis tool.22:T981,HEAT makes the iteration count at each approximation site learnable. For every nonlinear function replaced by an iterative solver, the paper considers the sequence of intermediate approximations F0, F1, ..., Fn produced by successive solver steps. The iteration count is discrete and not directly differentiable, so the authors introduce a learnable distribution over possible step counts at each site. Instead of choosing a hard step count during training, the forward pass uses the expected approximation under that distribution. This makes the objective differentiable with respect to both the halting distribution and the model weights.

To bias the solution toward shallow approximations, the loss includes a KL penalty that pulls each site’s learned distribution toward a negative-binomial prior whose mode lies at low iteration counts. Task loss pushes in the opposite direction when more precision is actually needed. The result is a site-specific equilibrium: many locations collapse to fewer iterations, while sensitive locations retain more depth. The method also uses activation-range regularization so that intermediate values stay in numerically favorable regions for approximation, reducing error amplification.

A key design choice is joint adaptation. The paper argues that merely learning per-site iteration counts is not enough. If weights remain fixed, many sites cannot be pruned aggressively because the original model was never trained under those approximation errors. HEAT therefore fine-tunes the pretrained model under the same approximations it will face at encrypted inference time, allowing the weights to absorb part of the error introduced by shallower solvers. This is the main distinction from prior FHE work that either hand-tunes approximation fidelity or redesigns the network to be encryption-friendly.

The training procedure is staged. The paper describes a three-phase schedule that first stabilizes the iteration distributions, then jointly optimizes distributions and weights, and finally converts the soft distributions into deployable discrete iteration counts. This staged recipe matters because fully joint optimization from the start would mix approximation-policy drift with weight adaptation and can be unstable. Implementation-wise, all Fi come from a single solver rollout, and backward recomputes intermediate states from checkpointed inputs, keeping memory overhead manageable.23:T630,The headline result is on encrypted GPT-2. HEAT reduces the total number of approximation iterations by 3.1×, lowers end-to-end decoding latency from about 54 s/token to about 38 s/token, and improves top-1 agreement with the plaintext parent model from 74% to 83%. That combination is important: the method is not simply trading away fidelity for speed, but recovering part of the lost accuracy through co-adaptation of weights and approximation budgets.

The most informative ablations compare three settings: fixed uniform iteration counts, learned per-site iteration counts without weight adaptation, and full HEAT with joint weight training. The gap between the second and third settings is central to the paper’s claim. Learning site-specific budgets helps, but joint training helps more because the model parameters internalize some of the approximation error. The paper also studies the effect of the KL regularization strength and the prior shape, which control the trade-off between preserving task quality and minimizing multiplicative depth.

Another useful empirical result is the learned heterogeneity across approximation sites. The model does not reduce all sites uniformly; instead, it allocates depth unevenly across layers and operations. That observation supports the main thesis that worst-case uniform approximation is systematically over-conservative. The site-level distributions are more revealing than the latency number alone, because they show that the gain comes from exploiting non-uniform error tolerance rather than from a generic compression trick.24:T4ad,Start with the introduction’s explanation of multiplicative depth and bootstrapping, because the rest of the method only makes sense once that bottleneck is concrete. Then read the part of the method that turns discrete iteration counts into differentiable halting distributions; that is the conceptual center of the paper. After that, focus on three things: the exact loss decomposition, the staged training schedule, and the ablation comparing fixed budgets, learned budgets only, and learned budgets plus weight co-adaptation.

For a skim-level read, 25–35 minutes is enough to capture the main idea. The most useful figures are the latency/iteration-count comparisons, top-1 agreement with the plaintext parent, and the per-site learned iteration distributions. If there is time, the appendix on approximation implementations and recomputation strategy is worth checking, because it determines whether the method is likely to scale beyond the reported setup. A good question to keep in mind while reading is whether the same recipe could be reused for other deploy-time discrete budgets, such as quantization levels, KV-cache compression settings, or expert-budget learning in MoE systems.25:T62a,实验基于 Petri 审计框架。作者为便于测量，将 Petri 改为无 rollback 的单分支审计，每个 transcript 限制 30 个 auditor turns；auditor 使用 Sonnet 4.6，并给 10,000 token extended-thinking budget。种子来自 Petri 2.0 的 181 条 seed instructions，过滤后剩 149 条，scaling 实验随机取 50 条。critique refinement 在 Sonnet 4.6 上效果最明显：cr2bo4，即 4 个并行 refinement loop、每个 loop 2 次 critique-refine iteration，使 realism win rate 从 10% 提到 26%，并降低模型显式表达“自己在被测试”的比例。其他模型也有提升：Opus 4.8 从 2% 到 6%，GPT-5.5 从 1% 到 6%，Gemini 3.5 Flash 从 8% 到 14%。增加 refinement iteration 或并行 loop 时，realism win rate 继续上升。与 compute-matched baseline 相比，作者把同等计算用于更多审计分支和更长 turn budget，例如 235 turns。Sonnet 4.6 上，选择 concerning score 最高的分支可把 concerning 从 cr2bo4 的 0.35 提到 0.42，但 realism 较低；若选择 held-out realism 最高的分支，realism win rate 只有 18%，低于 cr2bo4 的 28%，且 concerning 接近 baseline。DISH 在 coding-agent seeds 上也提升 realism：12 条 coding-agent seed 和 12 条 rephrase 中，Sonnet 4.6 从 9% 到 21%，Opus 4.7 从 1% 到 2%，Opus 4.8 从 2% 到 3%，GPT-5.5 从 0.1% 到 3%，Gemini 3.5 Flash 从 1% 到 3%。在 coding setting 中，两种方法可叠加；Sonnet 4.6 上组合方法比 DISH alone 再高 13 个百分点，其他目标模型也优于单独使用任一方法。26:T691,The experiments use the Petri audit framework. For cleaner measurement, Petri is modified to remove rollbacks, producing single-branch audits capped at 30 auditor turns. The auditor is Sonnet 4.6 with a 10,000-token extended-thinking budget. The seed set starts from 181 Petri 2.0 seed instructions; after filtering, 149 remain, and 50 are sampled for the scaling experiments. Critique refinement improves realism most clearly on Sonnet 4.6: cr2bo4, meaning 4 parallel refinement loops with 2 critique-refine iterations each, raises realism win rate from 10% to 26% and reduces verbalized evaluation awareness. The same setting improves Opus 4.8 from 2% to 6%, GPT-5.5 from 1% to 6%, and Gemini 3.5 Flash from 8% to 14%. More refinement iterations and more parallel loops further increase realism win rate. A compute-matched comparison is especially informative. When similar compute is spent on more turns and branches, up to 235 turns, selecting the branch with the highest concerning score on Sonnet 4.6 raises concerning behavior from 0.35 under cr2bo4 to 0.42 but yields lower realism. Selecting the most realistic branch by a held-out measure reaches only 18% realism win rate, compared with 28% for cr2bo4, while eliciting only baseline-level concerning behavior. DISH also improves realism in coding-agent settings. On 12 coding-agent seeds and 12 rephrases, it raises realism win rate from 9% to 21% on Sonnet 4.6, 1% to 2% on Opus 4.7, 2% to 3% on Opus 4.8, 0.1% to 3% on GPT-5.5, and 1% to 3% on Gemini 3.5 Flash. In coding settings, combining DISH with critique refinement gives additional gains; on Sonnet 4.6 the combination adds 13 percentage points over DISH alone.27:T565,The paper is a benchmark construction effort rather than a new model. TIC-Bench organizes evaluation into three dimensions and eight task types. Logical Association includes Linear, Cyclic, and Convergent structures, covering single-chain tracking, revisiting earlier scenes, and merging multiple reasoning branches. Temporal Association includes Sequential, Retrospective, and Parallel settings, testing forward event tracking, backward evidence retrieval, and coordination across concurrent storylines. Spatial Association includes Map and Photo reasoning, where models must reconstruct relative positions from fragmented visual patches and interleaved textual descriptions. This design is useful for two reasons. It does not merely increase the number of images; it explicitly controls reasoning structure, which helps separate perceptual difficulty from evidence-organization difficulty. And it reduces the chance of answering from parametric knowledge alone, forcing stronger dependence on local contextual bindings. The paper also includes error analysis, highlighting abstraction errors and reasoning errors as dominant failure modes. That distinction is informative: the issue is not purely in visual recognition or purely in language reasoning, but in the intermediate cross-modal state representation that must preserve entity identity and support multi-step composition.28:T51c,TIC-Bench contains 2,280 questions spanning three domains and eight task types. The evaluation covers 10 state-of-the-art MLLMs and 15 inference settings, including standard and thinking variants for several open models. Three findings stand out. First, the human-model gap is large: the strongest model, GPT-5.5, reaches 59.9% overall accuracy, while the human baseline is 91.7%. That suggests the benchmark is not dominated by annotation noise; models genuinely lack this capability. Second, difficulty is highly structured. In logical tasks, Convergent reasoning is harder than Linear and Cyclic, indicating that branch merging is a persistent weakness. In temporal tasks, Parallel reasoning is the hardest, pointing to difficulty in maintaining multiple concurrent evidence streams. Third, the error analysis shows that abstraction and reasoning errors dominate over pure perception failures. This matters because it means a model may recognize local visual content correctly and still fail by losing entity mappings, confusing character states, or failing to combine distributed clues into a final answer. The introduction does not expose the full per-model table, but it already makes clear that the benchmark’s discriminative power comes from reasoning structure rather than superficial task format.29:T4c4,There are three main design choices. First, the environment connects Civilization VI to an MCP interface through FireTuner over TCP and exposes 76 tools spanning state queries, unit control, city management, diplomacy, research, religion, trade, and lifecycle operations. This makes the agent operate in a tool-mediated setting rather than a fixed observation API. Second, the narration layer converts visual game state into structured text, but crucially does not automatically inject all relevant information into context. Information is representable, but not passively observed; non-local state must be explicitly queried. This allows the benchmark to distinguish unavailable information from available-but-unretrieved information. Third, the paper introduces behavior-level metrics instead of relying on leaderboard-style aggregate outcomes. PMR, Proactive Monitoring Rate, measures whether the agent actively checks latent strategic state such as victory progress or rival status. RAG@K measures whether a textual commitment is followed by corresponding actions within the next K steps. PMR captures whether the agent keeps checking the dashboard; RAG@K captures whether stated plans are translated into execution.2a:T563,The paper defines two drift settings. In StUi→U, the text is safe but the image contains unsafe content. In StSi→U, each modality appears safe in isolation, but their combination implies harmful intent. Using Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct, the authors manually inspect failures and separate them into two patterns. About 37% are visual risk unawareness, where the response follows the text instruction and largely ignores risky visual regions. About 63% are safety implication unawareness, where the model recognizes the visual object or scene but does not treat the request as safety-sensitive. The mechanistic analysis uses activation directions and attention on Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct and Gemma-3-12B-IT. The key observation is that explicit harmful text activates stronger safety-related directions, while visually grounded risk receives weaker attention and weaker refusal triggering. The proposed method, Safety-awareness Representation Transfer, extracts coarse safety-awareness directions from harmful-text processing near pre-refusal layers, refines them to balance safety and utility, and transfers them to cross-modal risky inputs while keeping the base MLLM frozen. Compared with adding safety fine-tuning data or relying on prompting, SRT is closer to representation-level steering aimed at repairing the weak link between visual risk perception and safety decision making.2b:T434,For foundation-model researchers, the main value is methodological rather than domain-specific. The paper presents a pattern that closely resembles modern pretraining infrastructure: canonicalize heterogeneous data into a shared contract, then connect diverse backbones through a thin adaptation layer and a common training recipe. That is directly relevant to multimodal pretraining, unified token-based modeling, and future AR-plus-diffusion training systems. One especially useful design choice is the explicit decoupling between source preprocessing and training-mixture construction. This mirrors good practice in large-scale language pretraining: do expensive normalization once, then iterate on filtering, sampling ratios, and source weights cheaply. Another useful lesson is the stage-wise training recipe. The authors argue that most optimization should happen during bidirectional training, while AR adaptation and DMD require relatively few steps. That resembles the broader pretrain → adaptation → distillation pattern seen in other foundation-model pipelines.2c:T62f,The method has two main components. The first is a multi-source data engine. SolarWM converts 10 datasets into a canonical training contract that aligns visual observations, camera geometry, language supervision, and quality metadata. The key point is not mere format conversion; it is standardizing temporal, geometric, and semantic supervision so that mixture design can happen at a common abstraction level. The pipeline explicitly separates source-level preprocessing from training-mixture construction: canonical clips are built offline once, and later mixtures are defined by configurable filtering, sampling, and source weighting. This makes reproduction and data-mixture iteration much easier. The second component is backbone-native adaptation. Instead of forcing all generators into a single internal representation, the framework adds only the shared interfaces needed for camera conditioning and long-horizon rollout, while preserving each backbone’s native latent representation, attention structure, and optimization objective. That is a pragmatic choice because video backbones are more heterogeneous than standard text Transformers. The training recipe has three stages: bidirectional model training, AR adaptation, and distribution matching distillation. The paper emphasizes that no specialized ODE or consistency-distillation initialization is required. The implied strategy is to learn most of the modeling capability in the first stage, convert it into an open-ended rollout form in the second, and use DMD as a lightweight final alignment and efficiency step.2d:T61b,On the data side, SolarWM processes 10 datasets into about 1.43 million canonical clips, corresponding to more than 25 TB of physical data, spanning real-world, synthetic, and game environments. On the model side, the paper instantiates four backbones at 5B, 14B, 22B, and 33B scales, based on generators such as wan2.2, ltx-2.5, and minimax-h3. Three experimental claims stand out. First, the models achieve strong long-horizon world-model performance without specialized ODE or CD initialization, suggesting that the unified recipe itself is effective. Second, most of the optimization budget should be spent in bidirectional training; AR adaptation converges quickly, and DMD needs even fewer steps. If the full paper provides step counts or compute allocation tables, those are worth close attention because they directly affect large-scale training design. Third, models trained only on 5-second sequences can still support open-ended rollouts over minutes to hours, without extra long-sequence finetuning or attention-sink mechanisms. If this result holds robustly, it suggests that long-horizon coherence may depend less on brute-force long-sequence training and more on the data contract, control interfaces, and rollout formulation. The abstract does not provide detailed benchmark numbers, so the most informative parts of the full paper are likely the ablations: multi-source versus single-source training, the effect of removing fields from the canonical contract, convergence differences across backbones, and the contribution of each training stage.2e:T47c,This is a skim paper, but it is worth reading with a systems lens. Start with the introduction and system overview to understand exactly what is unified and what remains backbone-specific. Then focus on the data engine section, especially the definition of the canonical contract and the separation between source preprocessing and mixture construction; that is the most transferable part for large-scale pretraining work. Next, read the backbone-native adaptation section and check whether the shared interface is truly thin enough to preserve pretrained backbone behavior. In the training recipe section, pay attention to the objective of each stage, the losses, the step allocation, and the transition criteria. For experiments, prioritize ablations over showcase videos or headline results: comparisons across data mixtures, backbones, and training stages are what determine whether the framework is genuinely useful. Around 35–50 minutes should be enough to extract the main ideas. If your focus is LLM or MLLM pretraining, read it as a paper on data engines and recipe engineering for the video domain, not just as a generative-video paper.2f:T7a7,The pipeline has four components. First is data curation. The authors collect 22,000 problems from 16 regional and international competition families plus online platforms, package them into executable evaluation environments, and explicitly exclude and deduplicate evaluation problems. They then use DeepSeek-V4-Flash to generate large synthetic reasoning-trace corpora: about 1.2M traces for the Nano model and 477,642 for the larger model. The key point is not just scale, but the organization of problems, reasoning, code, and feedback into long-context supervised examples.

Second is supervised fine-tuning. They start from Nemotron-3-Nano-30B-A3B and Nemotron-3-Ultra-550B-A55B and perform long-context SFT to obtain competition-specialized models. Nano is a 30B-parameter MoE with 3B active parameters; Ultra is 550B with 55B active. The supervision appears to target not only final code generation but also intermediate problem-solving traces.

Third is reinforcement learning. RL is applied to Nano-CC, with rewards derived from executable evaluation, namely whether the generated program passes tests and how much score it obtains. This reward is attractive because it is automatic and closely aligned with the task objective. The downside is that it may favor search-friendly behaviors rather than robust one-shot generalization. The paper places RL after SFT, which is sensible: the action space in competitive programming is too large for pure RL to be stable without a strong initial policy.

Fourth is GenCorrect, the test-time closed-loop strategy. It is more than self-consistency. The system generates diverse candidate solutions, evaluates them, uses the feedback to refine later attempts, and operates under a limited submission budget. This is essentially verifier-guided search plus iterative program repair. It is especially well matched to IOI-style settings, where multiple submissions and partial credit are part of the environment.30:T704,The main results are straightforward. On IOI 2025, Nemotron-3-Nano-CC improves from 130 Score@1 at baseline to 280 after SFT and 291 after RL. So SFT provides the dominant gain, while RL adds a smaller but real improvement. With five rounds of GenCorrect, the score rises to 468, above the gold threshold of 438.3. The size of that jump makes it clear that test-time search is a major contributor, arguably larger than RL in this setup.

Nemotron-3-Ultra-CC does not use code-specific RL. It reaches 304 with SFT alone, and 502.0 after five GenCorrect rounds. This suggests two things: larger base models still benefit from domain-specific SFT on hard coding tasks, and a strong base model plus strong inference-time search may be a more efficient path than a smaller model with additional RL.

The authors then build a competition-specific system for IOI 2026 and evaluate it live, before public release of the problems, under matched time, internet, and submission constraints. The system scores 535.4/600, above the gold threshold of 361.12 and above the top official human score of 498.27. As a research result, this should be interpreted carefully. It reflects a full specialized system, including task-specific adaptation and inference strategy, not just the intrinsic capability of the underlying model.

The most useful ablation insight is the gain decomposition. Synthetic data plus long-context SFT establish the main capability step. RL further aligns the model to executable objectives. GenCorrect then amplifies final benchmark performance when the environment allows iterative feedback. If one only looks at Score@1, the model improvement is much smaller than the final competition score, which is exactly why the distinction between parameter updates and test-time compute matters.31:T53b,This is a skim paper. Read it for pipeline decomposition, not for competition storytelling. Focus on three parts.

First, inspect the data curation and contamination-control pipeline. For foundation-model researchers, this is more interesting than the IOI headline because it shows how executable synthetic supervision is assembled, filtered, and kept evaluation-clean.

Second, read the tables that separate SFT, RL, and GenCorrect gains. Pay close attention to the gap between Score@1 and final multi-round scores. That gap tells you how much of the benchmark result comes from model updates versus inference-time search.

Third, read the GenCorrect algorithm and examples. It captures a broader post-training pattern: when the environment provides a verifier and actionable feedback, the model does not need to solve the task in one shot. It can iteratively repair its own outputs. That idea transfers beyond coding to math, tool use, and other verifiable domains.

A reasonable reading budget is 20–30 minutes. If you only want the research signal, read the introduction, method overview, main results, and ablations. Keep one framing in mind: this is not a pretraining paper. It is a strong post-training and inference-system paper whose value for pretraining lies in what it reveals about executable supervision and reward quality.32:T44d,DKL builds on task arithmetic. Given a base model θ_B and its instruction-tuned counterpart θ_I, the instruction-following vector is Δ_inst = θ_I - θ_B. New knowledge is learned by extended pretraining the base model on the target documents with the next-token objective, producing a knowledge vector Δ_know. The final model is obtained by adding the instruction vector and the knowledge vector to the base parameters, giving a model intended to retain instruction behavior while absorbing the new corpus. This avoids direct EPT on the instruct model, where instruction-following can be forgotten, and avoids generating massive synthetic QA for the entire corpus. A key implementation detail is embedding alignment: because the knowledge vector is trained from the base model but applied in a space involving the instruct model, token embeddings and chat-format tokens can introduce mismatch. DKL therefore uses the instruct model’s token embeddings during extended pretraining on the base model. Optionally, a small amount of synthetic QA supervision is added to improve recall at query time.33:T4f0,The method is deliberately simple. It follows a read-compute-feedback-reread procedure. In the first pass, the model reads the original task and long context, then produces one or more reasoning traces. These traces are not assumed to be fully correct; they are treated as an observable textual proxy for latent task state. In the second pass, the collected traces are serialized as text T and prepended before the long-context block C. The model then rereads the context and answers. This is trace as state. The critical control is trace append: the second pass uses exactly the same trace text, but places it after the long context. That makes the comparison unusually clean. Both conditions have the same information content, similar token budget, and the same two-pass structure; the only difference is whether the trace can influence contextual encoding during rereading. The theoretical section provides the conceptual backbone. Using causal state update processors with finite working memory, the paper shows a worst-case exponential separation between condition-first and condition-last orders. The result is not a tight theorem about practical Transformers, but it serves as a strong design principle for causal sequence models operating on long contexts.34:T431,The empirical study spans 3 frontier models and 3 long-context benchmarks, yielding 27 reported model-task-metric combinations. The models are DeepSeek V4 Pro Preview, GLM-5.2, and Qwen 3.7 Max. The benchmarks are GraphWalks 256K, MRCRv2 8-needle, and NUB-1M, covering different long-context reasoning regimes. The headline result is strikingly consistent: trace as state outperforms trace append in 26 of 27 combinations. That consistency is the main empirical message. The paper also runs targeted ablations on GraphWalks 256K with DeepSeek V4 Pro Preview to test alternative explanations. These controls weaken the view that gains come merely from generic trace scaffolding or from leaking first-pass answers into the second pass. Instead, the evidence supports the placement hypothesis: putting the trace before the context is what allows it to function as state during rereading. The full paper appears to emphasize robustness across tasks more than one single large absolute gain, and the effect is strongest on tasks where maintaining evolving task state is central.35:T7e8,The setup is deliberately controlled. All models are trained on English Wikipedia, about 3.7B tokens, with article-level topic labels used to define three domains: a forget domain consisting of Biology articles, about 3.7% of tokens; an adjacent domain including Medicine and Health, Chemistry, and Earth and Environment; and a retain domain containing the rest. The architecture is fixed across runs: a 254M-parameter GPT-Neo-style transformer with 16 layers, hidden size 1024, 32 attention heads, and MLP width 4096. In each layer, the authors designate a tiny biology subnetwork: one attention head and 64 MLP hidden units. Everything else is treated as the non-biology subnetwork.

To control entanglement, they repurpose Selective Gradient Masking during pretraining. For biology examples, gradients to the non-biology parameters are zeroed after backprop, forcing biology knowledge to flow mainly into the biology subnetwork. For a sampled subset of non-biology examples, the biology subnetwork is zeroed in the forward pass, training the model to handle retain and adjacent data without relying on that specialized pathway. The remaining examples are trained normally. By varying the fraction of training steps that use SGTM, they produce six models with graded levels of disentanglement. This is the central design choice: the dataset and the downstream unlearning algorithms stay fixed, while the internal routing of knowledge during pretraining changes.

They then apply three standard unlearning methods to every model: Weighted Gradient Ascent, Weight Divergence Regularization, and RMU. The logic of the paper has two stages. First, show that the six models indeed span a meaningful range of disentanglement, using three separate metrics rather than relying on naming or training settings alone. Second, compare retain cost at matched forgetting levels. This makes the experiment much cleaner than prior studies that varied data mixtures or unlearning procedures and then attributed the outcome to entanglement.36:T5e8,The main result is consistent and easy to interpret: more disentangled models achieve better retain-forget trade-offs. At a fixed level of forgetting, the most disentangled model incurs about 4x lower retain cost than the most entangled one under WGA and RMU, and about 1.3x lower under WDR. That is large enough to suggest that entanglement is not a minor nuisance variable but a substantial driver of collateral damage in unlearning.

The experimental scale is moderate but well controlled: roughly 3.7B training tokens, 1024-token blocks, a forget domain that is 3.7% of the corpus, and a fixed 20% subsample of each test split used for unlearning sweeps and reporting. All models have the same 254M parameter count, which removes model-size variation as a confound.

Two details are especially worth noting. First, the adjacent domain is explicitly separated from the retain domain, which makes the setup more realistic than a simple biology-versus-rest split. It allows the paper to probe whether forgetting one domain also harms nearby knowledge. Second, the gain from disentanglement depends on the unlearning method: WGA and RMU benefit much more than WDR. That suggests an interaction between representation structure and the mechanics of the editing algorithm, rather than a single universal effect size. For a closer read, the exact definitions of the disentanglement metrics and the retain-cost / forget-level measures are important, because they determine how far the conclusions can be generalized.37:T435,This is a good skim for methodology rather than for direct algorithm adoption. Start with the introduction, where the paper clearly separates localization from entanglement and explains why earlier work did not isolate the latter. Then read the experimental setup carefully, especially the forward-pass and backward-pass interventions used by SGTM. That is the core experimental manipulation. After that, inspect the section that validates the disentanglement ordering across the six models; without that, the later unlearning comparison is much less convincing. Finally, read the retain-forget curves for WGA, WDR, and RMU and compare how sensitive each method is to the underlying representation structure.

If you only want the main takeaway, 20 to 30 minutes is enough. If you want to connect it back to pretraining, two follow-up questions are worth thinking about: whether controlled specialization hurts general modeling efficiency, and whether softer routing or optimization constraints could induce similar disentanglement in larger, more natural pretraining settings.38:T521,The method defines a Bayesian model over hypotheses h.
- Representation: each hypothesis is a pair (natural-language description, Python implementation). Language provides a compressible, flexible concept description; code provides an executable semantics.
- Prior p(h): favors shorter NL descriptions (an Occam/MDL-like bias implemented in the language channel).
- Likelihood p(e_t|h): scores how well the program’s execution matches the observed evidence at time t.
- Inference: exact posterior p(h|e_{1:t}) is intractable, so they use LLM-augmented Sequential Monte Carlo (LLM-SMC-S). Maintain K particles; for each new observation, use an LLM prompt as a bottom-up proposal q(h_{t+1}|e_{1:t+1}, particles), then importance-weight by prior×likelihood and resample; optionally “revise” particles to better fit new evidence.
- Inquiry: because the particle set encodes posterior uncertainty, the agent can choose questions/experiments that best discriminate among particles (information-gain style), closing the induction–inquiry loop.
Key distinction from common LLM reasoning setups: the LLM is not the final reasoner but a proposal generator; correctness pressure comes from executable constraints and Bayesian reweighting, and uncertainty is represented explicitly via multiple competing hypotheses.39:T8e7,APEx maintains two levels of experience. Instance-level memory stores summarized execution traces for concrete queries, preserving operational details that can later be retrieved. Category-level skills aggregate many experiences within a category-modality bucket and distill them into reusable procedural guidance. At inference time, a query q is routed to a bucket, relevant memories R_M(q) and the corresponding skill R_S(q) are retrieved, and both are packed into a structured context E(q). The Planner uses E(q) to generate a research plan. The Executor follows that plan through multi-turn tool interaction and produces a trajectory τ and a final answer. After execution, the trajectory is summarized into a new memory entry, and once enough fresh experience accumulates, the Distiller updates the category-level skill.

The key idea is not just hierarchical memory, but the Executor-Distiller-Planner loop. Planner converts retrieved experience into a task-specific plan, Executor interacts with tools, and Distiller abstracts new trajectories into procedural skills. These modules are trained with a three-stage alternating GRPO schedule rather than a single joint objective. The motivation is cross-module credit assignment: if everything is optimized end-to-end at once, it is difficult for final task reward to shape the quality of distilled skills in a stable way. Alternating optimization gives each module a cleaner relative-reward signal while the others are partially fixed. In that sense, skill distillation is treated as reward-guided policy learning, not prompt-based summarization.

Another notable component is skill-guided TTRL. During deployment, the Planner performs test-time reinforcement learning on unlabeled queries, while retrieved skills and memories act as procedural priors that regularize online adaptation. The model is therefore not exploring from scratch at test time; it adapts locally around previously distilled high-quality procedures. The resulting improved trajectories are then written back into memory and can later trigger skill updates. This is the main conceptual difference from standard memory-augmented retrieval: retrieval helps the current sample, but APEx is designed so that the current sample also improves future behavior.3a:T64b,The headline result reported in the paper is an average score of 64.4% across deep research benchmarks. This is 14.7 points above GPT-5.4 and 3.0 points above the strongest memory-augmented baseline. For judging the method, the second comparison matters more, because it isolates the gain from experience organization and training rather than from a stronger base model.

The paper also highlights a context-efficiency result: a skill-only variant remains competitive while injecting fewer memory tokens. That supports the claim that category-level procedural skills are a more compact carrier of useful experience than flat trajectory retrieval. This matters for long-context agents, where the bottleneck is often not retrieval quality but context budget and retrieval noise.

From the full text, the experimental story appears to rely on three main ablation axes. First, replacing hierarchical experience with flat memory retrieval hurts performance, suggesting that instance memories and category skills play different roles. Second, removing reward-guided distillation or the alternating GRPO schedule degrades both skill quality and downstream task performance, supporting the claim that useful skills must be shaped by task reward. Third, removing skill-guided TTRL weakens online adaptation, indicating that test-time updates without procedural priors become high-variance exploration. The abstract does not provide all ablation numbers, so the main tables and appendix are the right place to verify effect sizes, benchmark composition, tool setup, and the exact definition of the no-ground-truth reward.3b:T454,This is a skim paper rather than a line-by-line read. Start with the introduction and focus on how the authors separate episodic, reflective, and procedural memory, and why they insist that distillation must be coupled to downstream RL. Then jump to the method overview figure and trace the data flow: query routing, two-level retrieval, the Planner-Executor-Distiller loop, and TTRL. After that, read the alternating GRPO section carefully enough to understand which module is optimized in each stage, where the reward comes from, and why this is expected to be more stable than joint training. Finish with the main results and ablations, checking three questions: does hierarchical experience beat flat retrieval, does the skill-only variant really save context, and is the TTRL gain robust.

If time is limited, about 30 minutes is enough to understand the design. If you want to go deeper, inspect the appendix for retrieval details, bucket construction, reward design, and memory-update triggers. Those implementation choices are likely to determine whether the method transfers to other agent settings.3c:T50c,The contribution is structured as definition → impossibility → quantitative characterization.
- Definition (epistemic Sybil resistance): marginal evidential contribution is measured by conditional mutual information I(Θ; Z | R). If it is zero, Z is merely an apparent new voice, not new evidence.
- Report-only identification barrier: different latent ancestry structures can induce the same observable report profile (including similarity/diversity patterns) yet require different Bayes-optimal posterior updates. Therefore, no aggregator that only sees report content can be generally correct.
- No-minting bound: descendants of fixed evidence cannot collectively contain unbounded information about Θ; there is a source-level ceiling.
- Gaussian shared-root model: reports are generated from a shared root signal plus extraction noise. The analysis shows (i) shared ancestry does not imply complete redundancy—repeated extraction can add information up to a ceiling; (ii) correlated extraction errors (e.g., agents sharing the same base model) accelerate saturation and reduce effective information gain.
- System implication: achieving epistemic Sybil resistance requires provenance interfaces or architectural constraints/estimation of dependence, not just text-based heuristics.3d:T7de,The method has four layers. First, coordination is modeled as a bilevel game. The orchestrator is the leader, workers are followers, and the orchestrator emits a decomposition tau over subtasks. Workers generate local solutions, while the system seeks to maximize a global utility U. Because real systems are not independent across workers, the paper introduces weakly coupled decomposition using an interaction graph, pairwise coupling terms psi_ij, coupling strength kappa, and maximum neighborhood degree d_max. Under bounded coupling, the follower game becomes an approximate potential game, and the equilibrium slack is controlled by decomposition quality. Better decomposition means smaller mismatch between local incentives and global utility. Second, reflection is modeled as stochastic movement over a discrete semantic memory state space. The paper derives finite-time upper bounds for free-form reflection under a one-sided drift condition, proves worst-case tightness, and shows that without an additional persistent-harm condition, unconditional reflection cannot guarantee arbitrarily low error. Third, the paper studies the role of verification. It constructs two environments with identical text-generation laws but opposite semantics for the same reflections. Any gate that only sees the transcript, even if randomized and history-dependent, must behave identically in both environments and therefore cannot improve both. A grounded verifier, by observing external execution or environment feedback, can distinguish them and recover geometric convergence. Fourth, the paper proposes SRMA, Stochastic Reflective Memory Ascent. A candidate memory is committed only if a fixed grounded evaluation protocol certifies a strict decrease in verifier risk. Under calibration and non-degenerate corrective mass, the method achieves exact geometric or polynomial convergence rates. A confidence gate handles stochastic probes, and re-anchoring restores segment-wise convergence under piecewise stationarity.15:["$","main",null,{"className":"flex-1 overflow-y-auto","children":["$","div",null,{"className":"mx-auto max-w-4xl px-4 py-8 sm:px-6 xl:max-w-6xl","children":["$","$L16",null,{"data":{"date":"2026-09-04","generated_at":"2026-09-04T01:46:26.061704+00:00","stats":{"total":101,"starred":8,"must_read":10,"skim":45,"glance":46,"by_category":{"pretraining_data":1,"industry":4,"llm_pretrain":1,"scaling":2,"architecture":2,"efficient":17,"eval":15,"multimodal":7,"multimodal_gen":4,"post_train":16,"mechanism":14,"coding_agent":3,"agent":15},"fetch_status":{"arxiv":"ok","huggingface":"ok","blog":"ok","twitter":"empty","github_trending":"ok","hf_trending":"ok"}},"categories":[{"id":"pretraining_data","emoji":"📚","name":"预训练数据","description":"以预训练数据为核心贡献的工作：数据采集/清洗/去重、数据配比(data mixture)、数据筛选/质量评估、语料构建、数据课程(data curriculum)、数据重复(repetition)、合成数据用于预训练、Tokenizer/词表设计、成员推断/数据检测。判据是『论文核心是数据本身』，而非训练 recipe/优化器(→ llm_pretrain)或评测资产(→ eval)","papers":[{"id":"2609.02343","title":"SonicCaps: Large-Scale Diverse and Fine-Grained Captioning for Improved Audio-Retrieval","abstract":"Recent advances in audio-language modeling have been driven by large-scale audio captioning datasets. Leaving that matrix unconstrained places no limit on the factor by which the mixing step rescales the residual streams, and that factor compounds across layers, which destabilizes training. Manifold-constrained Hyper-Connections (mHC) address this by restricting the matrix to the doubly stochastic matrices. That caps the factor","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2609_02672.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文处理 Hyper-Connections 在深层 Transformer 中的残差流混合稳定性问题。普通 HC 用无约束矩阵混合多条残差流，可能放大或压缩信号；mHC 用双随机矩阵避免放大，但仍会持续压缩流之间的差异，导致多条残差流逐层趋同。论文要解决的是：如何保留跨流混合路径，同时让混合操作在范数上既不放大也不衰减。","importance":"对预训练研究的启发主要在残差路径设计和深层训练稳定性。它不是新的语言建模目标，也不是数据方法，而是对 Transformer residual stream 的结构改造。价值在于把 HC / mHC 的训练现象用奇异值和均值-差异分解解释清楚：双随机约束只能防爆，不能防止多流表示坍缩；正交约束则同时保持范数和跨流信息交换。对想做更深模型、多残差流、动态残差混合的研究有参考意义。","technical_approach":"方法核心是把 HC 的残差混合矩阵约束到旋转群 SO(n)。HC 每层维护 n 条并行 residual streams，子层输出通过 post-mapping 分发回各流，旧状态通过 H_res 在流之间运输和混合。无约束 H_res 的最大奇异值可能大于 1，深度累积后会放大信号。mHC 把 H_res 投影到 Birkhoff polytope，即双随机矩阵集合，最大增益不超过 1，但最小增益没有下界，因此会收缩流之间的差异。论文引入均值-差异分解：双随机矩阵保持流均值，但会削弱差异分量；identity HC 不削弱差异，但完全关闭跨流混合。oHC 用 SO(n) 中的正交旋转矩阵作为 H_res，使所有方向的 s-gain 都为 1，因此不放大、不衰减，同时保留跨流路径。对 n=4 的常用设置，论文用一对单位四元数给出 SO(4) 的闭式参数化，避免 Sinkhorn 投影、Schulz 迭代或 Cayley chart 的额外开销。","experiments":"论文报告了几个关键现象。第一，在训练好的 mHC 模型中，几乎每个子层的混合因子都小于 1；跨 24 个子层复合后，整体因子降到 1.9×10^-14，同时残差流之间的相似度随深度上升。这个结果支持双随机约束会累积压缩 inter-stream differences 的分析。第二，identity HC 的混合因子恒为 1，流之间不再快速趋同，并且略好于 mHC。第三，oHC 在保持范数的同时保留混合路径，流之间比 iHC 更不相似，并在下游任务上进一步提高质量。效率方面，SO(4) 的四元数闭式构造比 Schulz 迭代、Cayley 参数化和 mHC 使用的 Sinkhorn-Knopp 投影更快。给出的内容没有包含完整任务表和绝对分数，因此更适合先看机制与消融，而不是只看最终指标。","reading_guide":"泛读建议 25-35 分钟。优先看 Section 3 的 s-gain、奇异值分析和均值-差异分解，这是理解全文的关键。然后看 Section 4，对照 Figure 1 和 Figure 2，看 mHC 为什么稳定但会让多流表示趋同，以及 iHC 为什么能作为一个重要对照。Section 5 值得看 SO(4) 的四元数参数化，重点是它如何保证正交、如何避免迭代投影。实验部分可重点看 HC、mHC、iHC、oHC 的消融，而不是只看最终分数。若研究方向是大模型主干架构或深层训练稳定性，这篇可作为 residual mixing 设计的参考；若只关心语言建模数据或 post-training，可快速浏览。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies stability in Hyper-Connections, where a Transformer keeps multiple residual streams and mixes them at every layer. Unconstrained mixing can amplify or shrink signals across depth. The doubly stochastic constraint used by mHC prevents amplification, but it still allows repeated contraction of differences between streams. The central question is how to keep explicit cross-stream communication while making the residual transport norm-preserving.","importance":"The contribution is mainly architectural and optimization-facing. It does not propose a new language modeling objective or a data recipe. Its value is in clarifying a failure mode of multi-stream residual designs: bounding the largest gain is not enough, because repeated attenuation can make the streams increasingly similar. For pretraining researchers interested in deeper Transformers, residual stream geometry, or dynamic residual mixing, the analysis gives a useful design principle: residual transport should control both expansion and contraction.","technical_approach":"$17","experiments":"The reported diagnostics match the analysis. In a trained mHC model, the per-sublayer gain is below one almost everywhere; composed across 24 sublayers, the factor reaches 1.9×10^-14. At the same time, similarity between streams increases with depth. Identity HC keeps the gain exactly one and preserves more inter-stream diversity, slightly outperforming mHC. oHC preserves norm while retaining the explicit mixer, keeps streams less similar than iHC, and further improves downstream quality. The SO(4) quaternion construction is also reported to be faster than Schulz iteration, a Cayley chart, and the Sinkhorn projection used by mHC. The provided text does not include full benchmark tables or absolute scores, so the main evidence to focus on is the stability diagnostic and the ablation among HC, mHC, iHC, and oHC.","reading_guide":"Skim time: about 25-35 minutes. Start with Section 3 for the s-gain definition, singular-value view, and mean-difference decomposition. Then read Section 4 together with Figures 1 and 2; this is where the mHC contraction effect and the role of iHC become clear. Section 5 is useful if implementation cost matters, especially the closed-form SO(4) construction with quaternions. motiva","starred":true,"priority":"p1","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2609_02737.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文解决的是长上下文解码时一个很现实的瓶颈：模型每生成一个 We observe that the only tokens a ranker must emit are the $N$ ordinal values naming the items in ranked order, and that this narrow, permutation-structured output format admits decoding strategies which are much more efficient than left-to-right generation. We introduce hLLM (Hungarian LL","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2609_01807.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI","cs.IR"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文解决的是生成式重排里一个很具体但很昂贵的瓶颈：LLM 已经能在上下文里联合比较候选项并生成排序，但输出阶段仍按自回归方式一个 token 一个 token 地吐出 ordinal，导致延迟主要花在 decode 而不是理解候选集合本身。论文的核心问题不是提升排序质量，而是利用“输出一定是一个长度已知、元素不重复的排列”这一强结构，把原本 O(N·T) 的顺序解码改成常数次前向完成的解码，同时尽量保留 AR 教师模型学到的排序能力。","importance":"对基座预训练的直接启发有限，因为它更像面向 reranking 的任务特化解码器，而不是通用语言建模方法。但它有两个值得注意的点。第一，它说明在输出空间高度受约束时，AR 解码未必是合理接口，很多信息可能已经存在于 prefill hidden states 中，只是被 AR 过程重新串行化了。第二，它把“从隐藏状态直接读出结构化输出”与“组合优化保证合法性”结合起来，这对代码生成中的约束解码、工具调用序列、以及某些 any-order/非 AR 生成任务有方法上的参考价值。若从 LLM 系统角度看，这篇文章更像是 decode interface redesign，而不是 backbone 创新。","technical_approach":"方法建立在一个很直接的观察上：重排任务最终只需要输出 N 个 ordinal，且每个 ordinal 恰好出现一次，所以目标不是一般文本，而是一个 permutation。作者据此把解码问题改写成线性分配问题。具体做法是，先用原 LLM 对用户上下文和候选项做一次并行 prefill，得到每个 item 的隐藏表示；然后接一个轻量 self-attention scoring head，把这些 hidden states 映射成一个 N×K 的 item-position score matrix M，其中 Mij 表示 item i 放在位置 j 的亲和度。最后不再用 AR 逐步生成 ordinal，而是直接在这个矩阵上跑 Hungarian 或 LAPJV 之类的最优匹配算法，求出最大权重的 item-position assignment，天然得到一个合法排列。\n\n这个设计有几个关键取舍。第一，作者不是从输出 logits 上做独立分类，而是从 prefill hidden states 读出位置分数，理由是排序所需的比较信息已经在 backbone 的自注意力层里形成，decode 只是把它逐步显式化。第二，合法性不交给模型学习，而交给组合求解器保证，因此不会出现重复 ordinal 或缺失 ordinal。第三，scoring head 设计得很轻，目的是把主要计算留在已有 backbone 上，避免为了省 decode pass 又引入一个同样重的 student。\n\n从全文描述看，论文还比较了几种 scoring head 变体，并专门分析 self-attention 层在提取 item 间相对关系中的作用。这一点很重要，因为如果简单 MLP 就够用，说明排序信息主要是 item-wise 的；而若 self-attention head 明显更好，则说明从 prefill states 到 permutation 的读取仍需要显式建模 item-item 竞争关系。作者的论证偏向后者，也因此把方法命名为 hLLM：核心不是普通并行分类，而是把排序解码视为 assignment。","experiments":"论文给出的 headline 结果是在专有数据和 Amazon Beauty 上，hLLM 相比 AR LLM 获得约 64× 的速度提升，同时保持接近的排序性能。文中一个很有代表性的延迟拆解是：带显式 reasoning trace 的教师模型单次请求约 1807 ms，其中 prefill 只有约 28 ms，说明绝大部分时间都耗在顺序 decode；如果能从 prefill states 直接恢复排序，理论上就能把主要瓶颈拿掉。无 reasoning 时教师 decode 长度约 39 token；有 reasoning 时约 657 token，这进一步说明生成式 ranker 的延迟会被输出格式和中间推理文本严重放大。\n\n实验部分的重点不是绝对精度数字，而是三类比较：一是 AR 教师与 hLLM 的质量-延迟权衡；二是不同 scoring head 配置的效果；三是对 backbone 与 self-attention 层贡献的隔离分析。作者声称在开放数据集上也复现了类似加速，说明方法不完全依赖专有业务分布。若按研究价值看，最值得关注的是“单次 prefill hidden states 是否足以恢复教师排序”这个问题，实验给出的答案是大体可以，但具体保真度仍取决于 head 设计和训练方式。\n\n需要注意的是，文中速度提升主要针对 reranking decode 阶段，而不是整个检索-重排系统的总延迟；另外 Hungarian/LAP 求解本身虽是多项式时间，但在常见 slate size 下远小于数百步 AR decode，因此在系统上是划算的。阅读实验时要区分这两层含义，避免把 64× 误解成端到端搜索系统的整体提速。","reading_guide":"泛读时先看 Introduction 和 Problem Formulation，抓住两个数字：prefill 很便宜，decode 很贵；输出是 permutation，不是自由文本。然后直接看方法部分里 score matrix 和 assignment formulation，这决定了整篇是否成立。实验里优先看 latency breakdown、与 AR 教师的质量对比、以及 scoring head 的消融。预计 20 到 30 分钟可以读完主线。\n\n如果想判断它对更一般 LLM 研究有没有借鉴意义，重点看作者如何论证“prefill hidden states 已经编码了排序所需信息”。这比具体 reranking 指标更重要。还可以留意 self-attention head 的必要性：如果它确实明显优于更简单的 head，说明结构化输出读取仍需要显式建模候选间相互作用。对做非 AR 解码、约束生成、或结构化输出接口的人，这部分最有参考价值。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses a very specific but expensive bottleneck in generative reranking. LLMs can jointly reason over a candidate slate and produce a ranking, but the output still has to be decoded autoregressively as a sequence of ordinal tokens. That means latency is dominated by sequential decoding rather than by understanding the candidates. The core question is whether the strong structure of the output—a known-length permutation with no repeats—can be exploited to replace O(N·T) autoregressive decoding with a constant-pass decoder while preserving the ranking behavior of the teacher model.","importance":"Its direct relevance to pretraining is limited, since this is a task-specialized decoding method rather than a new foundation-model training paradigm. Still, it carries two useful lessons. First, when the output space is highly constrained, autoregressive decoding may be an unnecessarily expensive interface: much of the needed information may already be present in the prefill hidden states. Second, the paper combines hidden-state readout with a combinatorial solver that guarantees output validity. That pattern could matter beyond reranking, for constrained generation problems such as structured tool calls, code with hard constraints, or some non-AR generation settings where legality matters as much as likelihood.","technical_approach":"$1b","experiments":"$1c","reading_guide":"$1d","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"将重排任务的输出约束（排列）与匈牙利算法结合，在 prefill 阶段直接输出排序结果，非常优雅的 non-autoregressive 改造，值得关注其在特定任务上的效率提升。","en":"An elegant non-autoregressive adaptation that combines the output constraints of reranking (permutations) with the Hungarian algorithm to output rankings directly during the prefill stage; highly relevant for task-specific efficiency improvements."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Emil Laftchiev","Prachi Agrawal","Moe Kayali","Bixing Yan","Qi Xu","Zijie Lei","Chen Qiu","Zhi Hua","Ke Li","Luke Simon"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["generative reranking","non-autoregressive decoding","Hungarian algorithm","LLM inference efficiency"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01807","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.01807","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2609.02083","title":"XMerge: Cross-Axis Selection and Reconstructive Layer Merging for LLM Depth Compression","abstract":"Removing complete transformer layers preserves a standard serving architecture, but existing depth-compression methods can lose substantial quality, and the loss varies unpredictably across models. We introduce XMerge, a post-training method with two components. Cross-axis selection identifies a block with low relative-magnitude and angular hidden-state change, and local boundary reconstruction re-fits the adjacent surviving block to match the original two-block output. XMerge uses no task label","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2609_02083.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文解决 LLM 深度压缩中的一个实际问题：直接删除 Transformer 层可以保留标准推理架构，但不同模型、不同层位置的质量损失很不稳定，已有方法很难同时选对要删的层并保留相邻层组合后的映射。","importance":"这篇工作属于推理效率和模型压缩，对预训练研究的启发在于它把层删除拆成两个可诊断环节：selector 决定删哪里，merge operator 决定如何吸收被删层的函数。这个拆分对理解 Transformer 层冗余也有价值。它没有改变宽度、attention 形状、词表或 serving 接口，因此比非结构化稀疏、宽度剪枝和插入替代模块更容易落到标准推理栈中。","technical_approach":"XMerge 包含两个组件。第一是 cross-axis selection。每个候选 block 用两个轴打分：relative magnitude 衡量该层造成的残差位移大小，Block-Influence 衡量输入输出方向变化，也就是表示旋转程度。两个分数在层间标准化后取 max，只有在幅度变化和方向变化都小的时候，层才被认为是较安全的删除候选。使用 max 而不是平均，是为了避免某个轴很大但被另一个轴抵消。第二是 reconstructive layer merging。方法不是简单丢层，也不是做解析式权重折叠，而是把相邻两层的原始映射蒸馏到一个保留下来的标准 Transformer block 中。具体做法是缓存校准输入下该层对的输入 h_l 和原始两层输出 h_{l+2}，然后优化保留 block 的全部参数，使其直接从 h_l 逼近 h_{l+2}。压缩后模型仍是 L-k 层的普通 Transformer，没有额外推理参数，也不需要任务标签或端到端微调。和 ReplaceMe、LLM-Streamline、LaCo/MKA/SWM/CoMe 等方法相比，XMerge 的关键区别是用一个已有的完整非线性标准 block 做局部重建，同时不引入新模块。","experiments":"实验覆盖 7 个 Llama 和 Qwen backbone，参数规模从 0.5B 到 主要降低局部函数映射损失。给定内容没有列出完整 benchmark 分数、延迟数字和各压缩率下的绝对性能，因此需要回到表格核对具体收益。","reading_guide":"建议泛读，约 20–30 分钟。重点看 Method 中的两个设计：RM/BI 两轴选择为什么用 max 合并，以及局部边界重建如何把两层映射压到一个标准 block。然后看实验中的 operator ablation 和 selector ablation，这两部分比主结果更能说明方法是否稳。若目标是服务端落地，还应看 cost 与 latency 分析，确认压缩后的 wall-clock 改善是否和层数减少一致。预训练研究员可以额外关注被判定为 quiet 的层分布，因为它可能反映不同 backbone standard, but quality loss is unstable across models and layer positions because a method must both choose a safe layer to remove and preserve the function of the removed layer plus its neighbor.","importance":"The work is mainly about inference efficiency and model compression, but it is also informative for studying layer redundancy in pretrained Transformers. Its useful framing is the separation of depth compression into a selector and a merge operator. The selector controls where compression happens; the operator controls how well the local two-block mapping is preserved. Since the compressed model keeps the same hidden size, attention structure, vocabulary, and serving interface, it is more deployment-friendly than unstructured sparsity, width pruning, or methods that insert replacement modules.","technical_approach":"$1e","experiments":"The evaluation spans seven Llama and Qwen backbones from 0.5B to 8B, five published baselines, and three layer-reduction levels. Then read the selector and operator ablations, which are more diagnostic than the headline results.
